SUPPLEMENT 2026-10-29 (the 7:14 note, under the translator's directive of the same morning — "we shall mimic the church's own American Bible footnotes in this regard," so that the work neither troubles the Church nor disparages Our Lady): a reader's note was inserted into the front matter, after the Yah note and before the Isaiah 1 heading, verse text untouched, modeled on the New American Bible's own footnote at this verse (verified at the desk against the USCCB text, 2026-10-29: the Church's American Bible itself reads "the young woman" in its text at 7:14 and carries "virgin" in its footnote as the Septuagint's rendering underlying Matthew 1:23). The note reads: "A note on Isaiah 7:14: The Hebrew word in this verse, 'almah, means a young woman of marriageable age; the word does not of itself speak of virginity. The ancient Greek translation rendered it by the word parthenos, which does mean virgin, and Matthew 1:23 quotes the verse in that Greek form. In the Catholic edition of this work, this verse reads "the Virgin.""
